# Competition Math Reference

A searchable reference for AMC 10/12, AIME and olympiad training. 753 cards
covering formulas, methods, and patterns (543 formulas, 166 methods, 44 patterns), plus a database
of 4,675 past contest problems (4,665 with their full statements), every one with a written
strategy and 4,071 cross-linked to the cards their solutions run through (2026-10-24).

Fully static and fully offline: no build step, no server, and nothing fetched at runtime. KaTeX
0.16.11 is vendored under `vendor/katex/` (its CSS, its JS, and the twenty woff2 faces the CSS
asks for), so the app renders the same on a plane or behind a network that blocks CDNs. It used to
load KaTeX from jsdelivr, where a block meant every formula in the library showed as raw LaTeX.

## Four kinds, four spaces

> Formulas tell you what is true; Methods tell you what you can do; Patterns tell you what you
> are looking at; Problems teach you to connect the three.

Every card is one of four kinds, and each kind has its own space, switched from the top bar:

| Space | Kind | Question | Colour |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Formulas** | one fact: a theorem, identity or standard result | What is true? | blue |
| **Methods** | one reusable move, made once you are inside a problem | What can I do? | teal |
| **Patterns** | one recurring problem shape, recognizable from the statement | What am I looking at? | amber |
| **Practice** | real contest problems, plus the study lists | Can I use it? | purple |

A pattern *suggests* methods, a method *uses* formulas, and problems practise all three
(derived from their tags). The test that separates the middle two: a pattern describes a whole
problem you can recognize from its statement, a method describes a move you make inside one.
CONVENTIONS §0 has the full model.

**Pages.**

- **Front page** (no hash): search, the four spaces, the Pattern → Method → Formula → Problem
  chain that says how they fit, links to every subject of every space, then where you left off,
  the problem and card of the day, and your library. The top bar is hidden here; Settings is
  reached from the gear in the top bar on every other page.
- **No main page for Formulas, Methods or Patterns.** `#/formulas`, `#/methods` and `#/patterns`
  (and the switcher buttons) open the subject last visited in that space, Geometry the first
  time. The small "i" beside the space's name in the sidebar opens a centered dialog, like
  Filters, with what the space is for and what was recently opened in it.
- **Subject pages** (`#/formulas/geometry`, `#/methods/algebra`): one scrolling page, every group
  (a formula topic, a method group, a pattern family) under its own heading and one-line
  `blurb`. A group's address (`#/formulas/geometry/circles`) opens the subject at that heading;
  so do the sidebar's group links. The sidebar shows only the current space; clicking the open
  subject's header folds it.
- **Problem pages** (`#/problem/<slug>`): a subject chip and the contest, the statement with its
  figures and credit line (AMC and AIME problems; other contests link out), then "How this problem
  works", which holds the strategy and the chain of cards the problem uses (Pattern → Method → Formulas).
- **Practice** (`#/practice`): a random problem by contest (or any) and optional difficulty, your lists
  (and a box to make one), every year of every contest, and the study lists. A contest year
  (`#/problems/aime/2024`) and a single list (`#/list/<id>`) open as plain pages with a link back.
- **Search** results are one list in order of relevance (formulas, methods, patterns, problems
  and study lists, each labelled), shown full width with no space highlighted. So are tag pages
  and special pages.

## Credits

AMC and AIME problems are © the Mathematical Association of America and are shown under
the [CC BY-NC-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-sa/4.0/) licence the MAA confirmed
by email (Oct 2026). The statements and their figures are read from [LIVE by Po-Shen Loh](https://live.poshenloh.com/past-contests),
which publishes every AMC and AIME problem with the MAA's permission; each problem page carries a one-line credit
under the statement. This app is non-commercial. `tools/fetch-live-statements.py` fetches them (statements and
answer choices only, never solutions or hints) into `js/data/problems/statements/<year>.js` and `js/data/problems/figures/`.
(AoPS blocks scripts with a Cloudflare check, so it is not used.)

Addresses are real, so Back, Forward and bookmarks work; a revisited history entry returns to
where it was left, a fresh click opens at the top. The first redesign's addresses
(`#/reference/...`, `#/solving/...`, `#/lists`, `#/problems`) redirect.

In the data, Methods and Patterns are one section each in `js/data/patterns.js` (group
`"tools"`), with the subjects as subsections and `groups` (each with a `blurb`) inside them;
formula topics carry their `blurb` on the subsection. `bookOf`, `SPACES`, `sectionHash` and
`getRoute` in `js/app.js` map all of it to the spaces and addresses above.

## Search

Hybrid retrieval, rebuilt from scratch and measured rather than tuned by eye.

- **Lexical**: BM25F over eight weighted fields (name, keywords, concepts, latex,
  description, section context, glossary, body), with fields combined as *strongest plus a
  fraction of the rest* rather than summed.
- **Phrase matching**: adjacent word pairs indexed with their own document-frequency
  table, including pairs from a spoken rendering of each formula, so "two pi r" finds
  the circumference.
- **Semantic**: static distilled embeddings (256-dim int8, ~17k words, four vectors per
  card), lazily fetched on first search and degrading silently to lexical-only if absent.
- **Fusion**: reciprocal rank fusion, gated on query length, lexical margin and semantic
  confidence, so short exact queries are left alone and paraphrases get help.
- **Spelling correction** resolves an unknown token to its nearest corpus word. Distance is
  optimal string alignment, so a swapped pair of letters ("descrates") costs one edit, not two.

Press `/` to focus the box, `Esc` to clear. A query that starts with `Special:` is a command
instead of a search (see Special pages below).

`tools/search-eval.html` runs 187 labelled queries against the live app and diffs every one
against `tools/eval-baseline.json`. Last run (2026-10-23, from the cloud session, after a full index rebuild): 161/187 top-1,
MRR 0.917. The index is now rebuilt in the cloud as well as on the Mac (PLAN, Blockers, has the
commands); the best recorded warm run, on the Mac with an older query set, gave 170/185, MRR
0.950, with the two probe sets at 64/78 and 48/77.
**Wait for the semantic vectors to load before reading results**, or the run under-reports
by a point or two; compare warm runs to warm runs.

## Other features

- **Contest tiers** label every card with where it is first needed: AMC (AMC 10 and 12 problems 1 to
  25), AIME Early (AIME 1 to 10), AIME Late (AIME 11 to 15), or Olympiad, and with its importance
  within its subsection (`high` down to `lowest`); the card's split tag shows both. One filter,
  behind the funnel in the top bar, narrows every list by tier and importance.
- **Problems**, reached from Practice by contest and year (AMC 8, AMC 10, AMC 12, then AIME), each
  linked to the cards its solution uses, with a pattern, method and formula chain and a strategy,
  shown at once or behind a button (Settings, Practice).
- **Study lists**, also on Practice, hold 36 curated sets alongside your own. The twelve study
  routes (AMC, AIME and Olympiad, each by subject) are *complete by construction*: every card
  of that tier and subject is in its route, which `tools/check-lists.py` enforces; the other 24
  are study lists on one theme. A list
  you build yourself is grouped into sections automatically, by matching its cards against
  the hand-written section headings already used across the built-in lists.
- **Diagrams** are computed from exact geometry rather than sketched, so every point is a
  true intersection, foot or tangency. Settings can show figures on the card faces too (off or on), and long write-ups place further figures inside the text, next to the
  paragraph each one illustrates (210 on 182 cards). Problems printed without a figure get one
  drawn for the app, labelled as such (282 so far).
- **Connected formulas.** A few broad cards (auxiliary lines, p-adic valuation, the mean chain,
  modular basics and fourteen more; 18 hubs, 96 links) end with a hand-picked row of the cards that are part of the
  same idea: LTE, Legendre and Kummer under p-adic valuation, the construction cards under
  auxiliary lines. It is curated and means "these belong to this card". Like Used by methods, it is
  a row of blue links that open a hover preview. The list is
  `js/data/connected.js`. (A computed "Related" row of nearby cards used to sit beside it; it was
  removed in October 2026 as more noise than help.)
- **Interactive widgets** on selected cards (75 geometry widgets you drag, 39 others you set): drag
  the vertices and watch the invariant hold.
- **Advanced search** builds a query from tag chips across the whole library.
- **Settings** (`#/settings`, the gear in the top bar), in panels:
  - Appearance: theme, density (Comfortable, Spacious or Compact: the spacing of the whole app), card
    size (regular, or large, one across), and text size.
  - Reading the math: **formula lines** (1, 2, 3, or All: the most rows of a card's formulas a
    browsing card shows; a card's own page always shows all), keyword tags, **notation** (in the
    expanded form, every formula box and Key forms bullet with a sigma or product sign shows its
    terms written out), and figures on card faces.
  - Browsing: subject pages (cards or a wiki-style A-Z index), card order (Curated or Most used),
    scroll speed, where the app opens, and the page-tools column.
  - Practice: the strategy on a problem page, shown at once or behind a button.
  - Commands, and **Your progress**: Export writes everything the app keeps in this browser
    (lists, stars, settings, theme, recent pages, flashcard and quiz progress) to one versioned
    JSON file (`competition-math-reference/progress`, version 1); Import merges its lists into
    yours and replaces the rest.

  On a wide window, a fixed preview panel on the right shows cards while browsing, a card's page,
  a subject page and a problem page as each setting changes, scaled so all four always fit.
- **Special pages**, run from the search box or from Settings → Commands, in the style of
  Wikipedia's: `Special:Random`, `Special:RandomProblem/<contest>`, `Special:RandomStarred`,
  `Special:WhatLinksHere/<card>`, `Special:AllPages` (an A-Z index with a letter bar),
  `Special:LonelyPages`, `Special:Statistics`, `Special:Search/<query>`,
  `Special:Browse/<filters>` (every card of a kind, subject, contest tier, or level, given in any
  order, as in `Special:Browse/Method/Geometry`), `Special:ProblemsUsing/<card>`,
  `Special:Unclassified` (problems still waiting for a card), `Special:Recent`, and `Special:NewCards`
  (the latest batch of new cards, in the order written; `Special:NewCards/All` lists every batch).
  `Special:Random` takes the same filters as Browse. Typing `Special:`
  opens a dropdown of the best matches, commands first and then their arguments (a contest, a
  card), which closes when you click away; every special page links back to the command list.
- **Experimental** pages, linked only from Settings, in `lab/`, opening in their own tab and loading only the data
  files so they cannot destabilise the app: a **Formula Web** that draws every card as a
  node and traces the shortest chain of authored links between any two, a **Quiz** that
  shows a statement and figure and asks you to name it, and **Flashcards**, a spaced-repetition
  review (a small SM-2: Again, Hard, Good, Easy) over any section or list, name to statement,
  statement to name, or both, scheduled separately per direction and kept in this browser.
- Light and dark themes; KaTeX throughout. Card faces carry the star and add-to-list buttons;
  Copy LaTeX and Copy Asymptote are on each card's own page.

## Running

```sh
python3 -m http.server 8688
```

then open `http://localhost:8688`. Opening `index.html` directly also works, except that
the semantic search index cannot be fetched from a `file://` page, so search falls back to
lexical-only. KaTeX is vendored, so math renders offline either way.

## Structure

Start with `CLAUDE.md` and `PLAN.md`. `PLAN.md` opens with "Current state": what is finished, what is in progress, and the next action. It is updated after every major change, so a new chat can pick up from it.

```
index.html                     page shell and script order
css/styles.css                 theme, layout, card and diagram styling
js/app.js                      routing, rendering, and the search engine
js/data/geometry.js            formula cards, one file per subject
js/data/algebra.js
js/data/number-theory.js
js/data/counting.js
js/data/patterns.js            the Methods and Patterns books, one section each
js/data/details/*.js           long write-ups, keyed by card id
js/data/examples-supplement.js worked examples, keyed by card id
js/data/diagrams/*.js          computed SVG figures, keyed by card id
js/data/problems/problem-db.js contest problem database: tags and strategies
js/data/problems/*-problems.js per-card practice questions and contest references
js/data/problems/statements/   problem statements by year, loaded on demand
js/data/problems/figures/      the statements' SVG figures
js/data/new-cards.js           the batches Special:NewCards lists
js/math-tools.js               the interactive "play with it" widgets on non-geometry cards
js/geo-interactive.js          draggable geometry widgets
js/search-semantic.js          semantic channel (loads js/data/search-vectors.*)
js/data/built-in-lists.js      curated study lists: routes, configurations, curiosities
js/data/connected.js           the Connected formulas rows on broad cards
lab/                           experimental pages (formula web, quiz, flashcards) + their shared loader
tools/build-search-index.py    offline: rebuilds the embeddings and glossary
tools/search-eval.html         search relevance harness
tools/validate-problem-db.js   checks every problem's card and topic ids resolve
tools/scan-conventions.py      the census behind CONVENTIONS.md; fails on a broken rule
tools/check-diagrams.py        every figure fits its canvas and no two labels collide
tools/check-labels.py          as rendered in Chrome: no label sits on a dot, a line or another label
tools/label-audit.html         the page check-labels drives; #show draws every offending figure
tools/check-lists.py           list ids resolve, every route is complete, Connected formulas resolve
tools/check-topics.py          every card carries a topic chip, and no subsection title awards a wrong one
tools/check-lab.sh             parse-checks the inline script of each lab page
tools/check-statements.py      every statement key and figure resolves, the credit header is present
tools/find-duplicate-forms.py  lists formulas stated on two cards (a report for rule 17)
tools/jsc-node                 stands in for macOS jsc under Node, for the JS checks
tools/gates.sh                 runs every gate above but check-labels, in order, stopping at the first failure
tools/bump-versions.py         bumps ?v= in index.html for changed files (--changed) and syncs the lab pages
tools/check-pages.js           browser check of pages: KaTeX errors, broken links, overflow, at 1280 and 375, light and dark
tools/add-cards.py             inserts a batch of new cards with write-ups, examples and registrations
tools/add-to-list.py           adds cards to a section of a built-in list and keeps its blurb's count true
tools/untagged.js              step D: the next problems without a write-up, newest paper first
tools/near-twins.js            step D: AMC 12 problems whose statement nearly matches a written AMC 10 one
tools/show.js                  prints problem statements by slug
tools/practice-set.js          writes problem entries from a JSON file of edits
tools/add-tag.js               adds a card to problems and to their AMC 10/12 twins
tools/live-cache/              LIVE's concept tags for every paper (hints only, never a source of tags)
```

Run every checker before committing (the list in CONVENTIONS §7); each gate exits non-zero on a
finding.

A card's write-up, examples and diagram are all keyed by its id in flat maps, so **moving a
card between files only moves the card object**; nothing else needs to follow it.

## Adding a card

```js
{
  id: "unique-kebab-case-id",
  name: "Display Name",
  type: "method",                            // omit for a formula; "method" or "pattern"
  subject: "geometry",                       // required on method/pattern cards
  latex: String.raw`a^2 + b^2 = c^2`,        // String.raw: no backslash-escaping needed
  description: String.raw`Prose; inline math with $...$ works.`,
  keywords: ["search", "terms", "here"],
  importance: "high",                        // high | medium | low | lower | lowest  (lowest = never used on a contest; lower = marginal use)
  tier: "AMC",                               // AMC | AIME Early | AIME Late | Olympiad
  level: ["MATHCOUNTS", "AMC10", "AMC12", "AIME", "Olympiad"]   // any subset
}
```

Drop it into the appropriate subsection's `formulas` array and reload; nav counts, search
index and filters pick it up automatically. Multi-formula `latex` separated by `\qquad`
(or `,`/`;` plus `\quad`) is stacked onto separate lines automatically, and
`\begin{cases}` / `\begin{gathered}` work for explicit layout.

The `subject` field matters: topic chips and the symbol-to-concept rules are gated on it,
so a method filed under Methods or Patterns still resolves against its home subject.

Longer content goes in the id-keyed side files: the write-up in `js/data/details/`, whose
headings depend on the card's kind (CONVENTIONS §2), and a `{ q, s }` pair in
`examples-supplement.js`. A leading `## Key forms` block is pulled out and rendered under the
description; each bullet may carry an explanation after a ` — `, shown as gray subtext. Register
the card in `tools/rewrite-register.json` and in a batch of `js/data/new-cards.js`
(CONVENTIONS, "New cards are registered").

If the `latex` uses `\sum` or `\prod`, add a `latexPlain` right after it with the terms written
out ($a_1 + a_2 + \cdots + a_n$); a Key forms bullet with a sigma writes both forms as
`\alt{sigma form}{written-out form}`. The notation setting picks one, and `scan-conventions.py`
fails on a sigma with no written-out twin. To give a broad card a Connected formulas row, add it
to `js/data/connected.js` (CONVENTIONS §12).

## Uploading to GitHub

The app folder is already a git clone of `Almond200/CompMath2.0`, so the steps are the same as in `CONTRIBUTING.md`: open it in GitHub Desktop (File, Add Local Repository), commit, and push; or run `git add -A && git commit -m "..." && git push` in Terminal. Do not drag files into a second folder (Finder moves them), and do not use the web uploader once there are more than 100 files.

The fact stays text only (a diagram was tried and dropped at the owner's request).
- **Two practice problems is now a gate (2026-10-25).** At the owner's request: every card, new ones included, must have at least two practice problems. `node tools/practice-gaps.js --check` fails otherwise and runs in `tools/gates.sh`. The new cards already meet it (Comparing Coefficients has 11 linked problems, Rolling Without Slipping 8, Tethered Animal 2; all 756 cards have two or more). CONVENTIONS gains §3b (what counts as a practice problem: a real route first, exercises only as the fallback) and a new-card checklist; CLAUDE.md and QUESTIONS.md point to them, and CLAUDE.md records what a "tag" is and that the search index is rebuilt once per step.

The statement box has no outline: a soft tint between white and the hover blue (`--daily-box`), slightly whiter than the panel when it is hovered. The pick is drawn from the AMC and AIME problems, whose text is stored; `showStatement()` in `js/app.js` fills either the problem page or the panel.

Cards added where a route used them uncited include Quadrilateral Area from Diagonals on 2013 AMC 10A #16, Graph Coloring on the ring of 2016 AIME II #12, Unfolding a Surface on the cone of 2004 AIME II #11, the rotation trick on 2006 AMC 12B #23 and Naming a Repeated Block on 1983 AIME #3. No new candidate card.
- **Coloring Arguments broadened (2026-10-25).** At the owner's request the card now also covers checkerboarding a grid figure just to see it: the cells of each color often form a simpler shape of their own. The write-up gains a step and a paragraph (a diamond of cells is two interleaved square arrays turned $45^\circ$, one per color), and 2000 AMC 12 #8 with its twin 2000 AMC 10 #12 gains that route as its "Alternatively" (the tips' color holds $(n+1)^2$ squares, the other $n^2$; checked by computation for $n\le7$). Also from the pass over problems citing two or more cards: the AMC 8, AJHSME, HMMT, AMC 10 and AMC 12 are read, with about ten cards added where routes used them uncited.

With that every under-tagged AMC 8, AJHSME, AMC 10, AMC 12 and AIME problem has been read. The semantic index is rebuilt for the $756$ cards (eval $160/187$ top-1, MRR $0.910$).

Most of the untagged ones are plain arithmetic and stay so; about $50$ routes gained the card they use, among them fencepost counting (seven term and gap counts), area by pieces, joined shapes on glued cubes, the folded sheet, Varignon on midpoint squares, round-robin counting, logical deduction on arrangement puzzles, constructive counting and casework. No new candidate card.
- **Three new cards, P39 to P41 (2026-10-25).** Approved by the owner and written: the method Comparing Coefficients (an identity in $x$ or in a parameter holds power by power, with the proof that a polynomial of degree at most $n$ with more than $n$ roots is zero, and the common point of a family of lines), the pattern Rolling Without Slipping (a coin of radius $r$ around a circle of radius $R$ turns $\tfrac{R\pm r}r$ times, with a figure), and the pattern An Animal Tethered to a Building (sectors around each corner, with a figure: $88\pi$ for a $10$-foot rope at a corner of a $6\times4$ shed). Each has a worked example checked by computer, joins the AMC routes, and is cited by the problems that needed it: nine for Comparing Coefficients, seven for rolling, two for the tether. **2026-10-24**

- **E: second pass, second batch (2026-10-24).** Pick's Theorem now has a full proof (additivity, rectangles, right triangles, all polygons); Binet's card proves the sum of squares, Cassini, the addition rule and the gcd property; Menelaus proves its converse; tangents to conics derive the parabola form and the director circle; trees prove all three "two of three" pairs; parametric conics, the parabolic segment, committee-and-chair, 3D distance and the right-triangle inradius completed. The Handshakes card's main box now also shows $\binom n4$ for the diagonal crossings (owner). Commits now come about every 30 cards (owner). Thin cards left: 0.

Full proofs also for the Matrix–Tree Theorem, Minkowski (Blichfeldt's step), coprime density (Möbius), Wolstenholme's binomial forms, the resultant's vanishing criterion (product formula quoted), Cardano, Stirling and floor reciprocity. `tools/proof-depth.js` now lists no thin card. Cards under two: 277.
- **F: 17 more Medium cards (2026-10-24).** Simson line, symmedians, Fermat point, Apollonius circle, cyclic-quadrilateral tests, Brahmagupta, perpendicular diagonals, center coordinates, section formula, conic classification, tangents and parametric forms, Cavalieri, the tetrahedron centroid, Pascal, pole and polar, harmonic bundles. Cards under two: 295.
- **F: 16 more Medium cards (2026-10-24).** Fixed points, trees, the planar edge bound, Hall and Kőnig, triangle type from the sides, the incenter's area split, incircle and excircle touch points and homothety, half-angle identities, altitude and bisector, Ceva (Gergonne point and altitudes), the perpendicularity lemma, the contact triangle and the Euler line. Cards under two: 312.
- **F: 24 Medium cards (2026-10-24).** Practice exercises for symmetric polynomials, Lagrange interpolation, quadratic forms, inverse functions, matrices (products, Cramer's rule, inverses), $a^n \pm b^n$, the SOS identity and other factorizations, Bernoulli, Jensen and power means, angle values, arctangent telescoping, proportions, grid rectangles, nested subsets, squared binomial sums, committee-chair, Stirling numbers, tail sums and total expectation. Five drafts that repeated the card's own example were rewritten. Cards under two: 328.
- **F: the High-importance cards (2026-10-24).** The eleven High cards short of two practice problems now have them, as supplementary exercises in `extra-problems.js`: determinants, the anticomplementary triangle, the nine-point circle, hyperbolas, stretching one axis, the area method, polynomial division, GCD substitution and the three proof methods. Cards under two: 352.
- **D2 finished; F begun (2026-10-24).** Every "no overlap" flag of the tag audit is read (2000 to 2006, AIME 1983 to 1999 and AJHSME added: one fix, 2004 AMC 8 #18 now cites Casework like its twin puzzle 2021 Spring AMC 10B #17). No AMC problem cites a Lowest-importance card. New `tools/practice-gaps.js` counts each card's practice problems: 363 of the 743 cards have fewer than two.
- **D2, tag audit 2007 to 2026 (2026-10-24).** The "no overlap" flags of `tools/audit-problem-tags.py` were read for every paper from 2007 on; nearly all are cards the write-up uses under another name. Fixed: 2023 AMC 8 #7 (lines, not distance), 2021 Fall AMC 10A #6 / 12A #5, 2016 AMC 10B #11 / 12B #9 and 2013 AMC 10B #4 / 12B #3 (fencepost counting, not arithmetic series), 2019 AMC 10B #25 / 12B #23 (binomial coefficients, not the Binomial Theorem), 2008 AMC 8 #5 (average speed, not palindromes) and 2007 AMC 10B #8. Plain least-common-multiple problems cite GCD × LCM, whose fact is $\gcd\cdot\operatorname{lcm}=ab$; a card for them is proposed as P38 in QUESTIONS.md.
- **D3, third group (2026-10-24).** 63 more entries tagged where the write-up uses the card: averages to totals and mean, median and mode (23), the Pythagorean theorem (18, from slant heights to tangent-circle gaps), working backwards, counting shapes, last-digit patterns, tiling a board and completing the square. The law of cosines and sines, Heron's formula, harmonic-mean and clock lines LIVE lists were read and left, since those routes do not use the card. The 253 word-for-word AMC 10/12 twin pairs all carry matching tags. Problems citing no card: 658.
- **D3, second group (2026-10-24).** 45 more LIVE-guided tags, each checked against the write-up: relative motion (2004 AMC 10A #17, 2006 AMC 10A #15, 2008 AMC 12B #20), ages, special right triangles, palindromes (2021 AIME II #1, 2023 AIME II #2, 2026 AIME I #2), cryptarithms, defined operations, truth-tellers, geometric distribution, De Moivre, difference of squares, reading graphs and tables (seven AMC 8 and AJHSME problems) and others, twins included. The matcher is now `tools/live-gaps.js`. Problems citing no card: 674.
- **D3 begun: LIVE-guided labelling (2026-10-24).** `tools/live-cache/` concepts that name a card (Vieta, inscribed angle, cyclic quadrilateral, telescoping, inclusion-exclusion, complementary counting, independent events, double counting, lattice paths and others) were matched against each problem's tags; every problem with such a concept but not the card was read, and the card was added only where the written route uses it: $43$ tags, among them Vieta on 2009 AMC 12A #16, 2010 AMC 10A #19 / 12A #17, 2015 AIME I #10 and 2023 AIME I #9; inscribed and cyclic angles on 2016 AMC 8 #23, 2016 AIME II #10, 2019 AIME I #13 and 2020 AIME II #15; Length of a Chord on 2011 AIME II #10; telescoping on four products; double counting, inclusion-exclusion and complementary counting on AMC 8 and AJHSME problems. The LIVE concepts were hints only: no tag or text came from them.
- **Classifying problems that cite no card, AMC 10 (owner, 2026-10-24).** Every AMC 10 entry without a card was read and given the card its route uses where one honestly fits (the twin AMC 12 entry gets the same): Mean, Median, Mode; Solving a Linear System by Elimination; Logical Deduction and Truth-Tellers; Coins and Prices; Setting Up a Proportion; Estimating to Choose an Answer; Simulating the Process; Fitting Copies Together; Rotating a Point; Magic Squares; Absolute Value Rules; and others, about 80 entries in all; then the AMC 12 entries without an AMC 10 twin (45 more: Counting Solutions by Graphing, Coefficient Sums, Digit Manipulation, Counting Multiples, Reflecting a Point, Forms of a Line, Invariants, Pairing Elements to Bound a Set, and others) and 2013 AIME II #1; then the AMC 8 (about 190 entries: Reading Graphs and Tables, Setting Up a Proportion, Logical Deduction, Coins and Prices, Folded Paper, Painted Cubes, Telescoping, Time per Leg, Relative Motion, and others). Then the AJHSME (31 entries). In all, 999 entries without a card became 688; the rest are plain arithmetic or need a card that does not exist yet, and nine such ideas are now candidates P29 to P37 in `QUESTIONS.md` (cube nets, elimination counting, area by adding and subtracting, rounding up to whole items, views of cube stacks, symmetries of a figure, joining shapes, assuming all one kind, and multiplying equations). 1989 AJHSME #13 had lost a "$10$" to shell expansion when it was written; it is restored, and no other strategy has an unbalanced dollar sign.
- **D2: calendar cards, named cards, heavy cards (2026-10-24).** Ten day-of-the-week entries now cite the specific Calendar Problems card instead of Congruence Rules (2002 AMC 8 #5, 2008 AMC 8 #3, 2019 AMC 8 #14, 2002 AMC 10B/12B #8, 2012 AMC 10A #12 / 12A #9, 2016 AMC 10B #4, 2000 AMC 10 #25 / 12 #18). Write-ups that name a card they did not tag now tag it: the $13$-$14$-$15$ triangle (2026 AIME I #10, 2013 AMC 10B #23 / 12B #19, 2000 AMC 12 #19), the Shoelace Formula (2007 AIME I #13, 2017 AMC 12B #24), CRT (2024 AIME II #14) and AM-GM (2016 AMC 10B #17 / 12B #15). Two AMC problems solved only by an Olympiad tool now lead with the AMC route and keep the tool as \"Alternatively\": 2014 AMC 10B #17 factors $5^{1002}-1$ as a difference of squares (LTE second), and 2011 AMC 12A #23 composes the Möbius map and uses trace zero for an involution (eigenvalues second).
- **D2 begun: twin entries and out-of-tier tags (2026-10-24).** A scan of every AMC 10/12 near-twin (same year, statements over $85\%$ alike) found $8$ shared problems whose two entries had drifted apart; $6$ now share one entry (2007 #17/#14, 2021 Spring 10A #14/12A #12, 10B #12/12B #7, 10B #20/12B #15, 2023 #18/#15, 2025 #13/#10), the chosen text ending with its choice letter, and the other $2$ differ only in the operator symbol each statement prints. 2022 AMC 10A/12A #1 share one entry: evaluate inside out, with the continued-fraction convergents as the \"Alternatively\" route. Of the $31$ AMC tags on Olympiad-tier or Lowest cards, 2011 AMC 10B #24 / 12B #19 now reach $\tfrac{50}{99}$ through the Farey-neighbour step they cite; the others are used by their routes.
- **Step D finished: 1988 to 1985 AJHSME (2026-10-24).** The last $100$ problems written and checked by script, so all $4{,}675$ problems in the database have a strategy. 1988 #16 shows two empty squares break at most $7$ of the $8$ lines ($6$ X's); #24 turns the square $150^\circ$ at each hexagon corner ($450^\circ$, a quarter turn); #25 counts $54+3$ palindromes. 1987 #7 counts $8$ corners, $8$ edge cubes and $4$ face centers ($20$); #24 needs $5c-2w=48$ with $c+w\le20$ ($12$). 1986 #9 counts routes backwards from $N$ ($6$); #21 folds each added square ($A,B,D,E,F,H$); #19 uses only the gas bought after the start ($\tfrac{860}{32}$). 1985 #20 finds the three days that occur five times (Wednesday); #24 adds the three sides ($3S=75+42$).
- **Step D: 1994 to 1989 AJHSME (2026-10-24).** All $150$ written and checked by script, figures read before solving: 1994 #4 turns the shapes one place clockwise; #24 counts the colorings with a green square forcing its upper and right neighbours ($6$); #25 writes the product as $4\cdots4\,0\cdots0-4\cdots4$ ($846$). 1993 #20 counts $91$ nines and $07$ ($826$); #25 tilts the card $45^\circ$ to touch $12$ squares (checked by a sweep over positions and angles). 1992 #20 folds each net (D puts two squares on one face); #21 compares the bars as ratios (February, $\tfrac53$). 1991 #16 tracks the stacks through four folds ($9$); #24 fits one $2$-cube and $19$ unit cubes. 1990 #10 reads the calendar two weeks down ($P$); #25 lists the $8$ two-square patterns. 1989 #20 takes one face from each opposite pair ($6+5+3$); #23 paints $9+12+8+4=33$. $100$ AJHSME problems remain (1988 to 1985).
- **Step D: the Fall 2021 AMC 12 finished (2026-10-24).** The $26$ problems without an AMC 10 twin written and checked by script, with figures drawn for 12A #11 and #24 and 12B #8, #9, #22 and #24: 12A #15 notes the conjugates are the same roots, so $Q(w)=256P\left(\tfrac w{4i}\right)$ ($208$); 12A #16 isolates one brand A computer ($190+1$); 12A #22 subtracts the $X$ lines left beside each $O$ line ($148$); 12A #24 finds the two trapezoids $(16,14,12)$ and $(8,13,3)$ ($66$); 12A #25 shifts quadruples so each sum class gets $\tfrac1m$ of them ($(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)$, $c_1=11$); 12B #16 shows the gcds are pairwise coprime ($438$); 12B #18 turns the recurrence into $w_{k+1}=w_k^2$ ($k=10$); 12B #24 gets $AF=AC$ from two powers ($CF=30$); 12B #25 needs the divisors of $n+1$ in $2..10$ to add to $9$ ($13$, $97$). Every AMC 10, AMC 12 and AIME problem has a strategy. 12A #16 keeps `formulas: []`; its fact is the new candidate P28 (Most Edges in a Disconnected Graph) in `QUESTIONS.md`.
- **Step D: the 23 Fall 2021 AMC 12 twins (2026-10-24).** `tools/near-twins.js` matched 12 problems of 12A and 11 of 12B with Fall AMC 10 problems; each pair was read side by side (and 12B #2's figure compared with 10B #2's), and each AMC 12 entry is a copy of its AMC 10 entry. 26 Fall AMC 12 problems remain.
- **Fall 2021 AMC 12 statements imported (2026-10-24).** LIVE publishes the Fall 2021 AMC 12A and 12B as its 2021 AMC 12 \"C\" and \"D\" papers (the owner spotted them). `tools/fetch-live-statements.py` maps the Fall 12A and 12B there, and its new `--only <contest/paper,...>` fetches just those papers and merges them into the existing year file. All 50 statements and their figures are in; 12B #23 is now credited to LIVE instead of the AoPS Wiki. A forced space `\ ` before a closing `\)` (12B #10) no longer escapes the closing dollar sign. `tools/gates.sh` no longer pipes `check-statements.py` through `tail`, which had hidden a failure and still printed ALL GATES PASSED. Every problem in the database now has its statement (4,665 of 4,665).

- **Curated lists brought up to date (owner, 2026-10-24).** Every non-route list was checked against the cards added since 2026-10-12. Inequalities Ladder gains Maximizing a Product, the Absolute Value Triangle Inequality, Popoviciu, Vornicu–Schur and Nesbitt, plus two new sections: Inequalities in Geometry (the triangle inequality, Ptolemy, Weitzenböck, Erdős–Mordell, Euler, Ravi) and Ways to Prove One (SOS, the tangent line trick, normalization, smoothing, uvw, derivatives). New sections elsewhere: Olympiad Triangle Lemmas (Deeper Configurations), Everyday Counts (Counting Setups), Winning Games (Invariants), and Polynomials at Olympiad Level (Olympiad Heavy Hitters). About 45 recent cards joined existing sections, including the four new methods in the Algebra Core, and the stated card counts were corrected.

- **Step D: 2019 AMC 12A finished, 2019 AMC 12B begun; a better twin finder (2026-10-24).** 2019 AMC 12A #21 ($k^2\bmod8$, product $36$), #22 (coordinates, $s=\tfrac{80}{\sqrt{13}}-\tfrac{34}{\sqrt3}$, with a figure zoomed on the triangle), #23 (telescoping to $\log_2n$) and #25 (deviations from $60^\circ$ multiply by $-2$; $n=15$); 2019 AMC 12B #3, #8, #9 and #10 (the unused roads form a forced perfect matching; $4$ routes). `tools/near-twins.js` now scores shared 6-letter runs instead of a 50-character prefix: it found 2019 AMC 12B #4, #5 and #20 to be 2019 AMC 10B #6, #7 and #23 (copied), and 20 more twins in older papers.
- **Step D: 2020 AMC 12B finished, 2019 AMC 12A begun; search index rebuilt (2026-10-24).** 2020 AMC 12B #22 names $s=t/2^t$ (maximum $\tfrac1{12}$), and #25 finds the region by sum-to-product and uses AM–GM ($2-\sqrt2$). 2019 AMC 12A #1, 2, 12, 13, 14, 15, 17, 19 are written, all checked by script ($432$ colorings; $(m+1)^2+(n+1)^2=202$; sides $3:2:4$). The semantic index covers all 742 cards: 161/187 top-1, MRR 0.917.
- **Step D: 2020 AMC 12B, ten problems (2026-10-24).** #1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 10, 12, 13, 17, 20 written and checked by script, with figures for #10 and #12: #10 by the power of $A$ ($AP\cdot AM=\tfrac14$, $AP=\tfrac{\sqrt5}{10}$); #12 shows $CE^2+DE^2=CD^2-2\,CE\cdot DE=100$ with $OE$ cancelling; #17 forces the roots $r,\omega r,\omega^2r$ with multiplicities $(1,2,2)$ or $(3,1,1)$; #20 sums the squared class sizes, $\tfrac{588}{4096}=\tfrac{147}{1024}$.
- **Four method cards the owner asked for, and a new list (2026-10-24).** Transforming Equations Without Losing Solutions (equivalent and one-way steps, the zero case, sign conditions before squaring, substitution ranges, parameters; it holds the radical-equation routine), Polynomial Long and Synthetic Division, Locating the Roots of a Quadratic Without Solving, and Directed Lengths and Sign Checks (with a figure). Every example was checked by script. Waiting for Success gains the $r$th success (negative binomial, $\frac rp$ trials, $\frac{r(1-p)}p$ failures). The cards join the AMC routes and are linked from Menelaus, Orthocenter, Euler Line Parallel to a Side, Power of a Point, Logarithm Rules, Partial Fractions, Substituting $y=x+1/x$, Vieta and the Remainder Theorem. A new thinking list, Checking Your Work, gathers the moves that keep every solution and test the answer. 742 cards.
- **Reported errors checked and fixed (owner, 2026-10-24).** Each claim was checked by computation before editing, and each card face now agrees with its write-up. Wald's identity: a stopping time needs independent terms (counterexample: $X_i=B$ and $N=1+B$ give $1$, not $\frac34$). Basic Probability: only favorable over total needs equally likely outcomes. LGV: the identity-matching condition is now beside the determinant. Regions: a circle with $k\gt0$ crossings adds $k$ regions. Variance: independence is enough but not needed. Graph Coloring: bipartite iff $\chi\le2$, and Brooks needs a connected graph. Fencepost: a short last gap gives $\lceil L/d\rceil+1$, and the 8 Mondays line is fixed. Sprague–Grundy: the reverse statement corrected. Cauchy example: the solution no longer relies on $c^x$. Matrix–Tree: the eigenvalue form is stated for connected graphs. Vieta: the root gap is now $\sqrt{\lvert\Delta\rvert}/\lvert a\rvert$. Menelaus: the converse needs directed lengths or a count of the points outside the sides. First-order recurrences: a classification table. Euler line parallel to a side: $AH=2HD$ only for acute triangles (counterexample given). Focal radii: $PF=\lvert y+p\rvert$, and the hyperbola's latus rectum is now written $c^2/a-a$. Circle-power families: real only when $r_t^2\ge0$. Lester: the false Kiepert and $60^\circ$ claims replaced. Soddy–Gosset and Kruskal–Katona are labelled as quoted, with references. Strategies: 2002 AIME I #3 finishes the count ($36-8-3=25$), and 2025 AMC 10B #21 / 12B #17 show the complete case analysis (4 classes of sizes $8,8,4,8$).

**2026-10-23**

- **Settings: "Where I left off" removed (owner, 2026-10-23).** When the app opens now offers Front page or Formulas; a saved "Where I left off" opens Formulas. The app no longer records the last page (`mq-last`), clears the old key on load, and leaves it out of the progress export.
- **Step D: 2020 AMC 12A finished (2026-10-23).** The $14$ unwritten problems written and checked by script, with figures for #14 and #24: #2 and #6 are read off their figures ($13+4\sqrt2$; orbits of $4+2+4$ squares, $7$ more); #9 counts five branches of $\tan2x$ ($5$); #12 cites the new rotated-line card, since the center lies on the line (slope $4$, $x$-intercept $15$); #13 gives $\tfrac1a+\tfrac1{ab}+\tfrac1{abc}=\tfrac{25}{36}$ ($b=3$); #21 compares exponents prime by prime ($48$; a first brute force stopped at $10^6$ and said $36$, but the largest such $n$ is $18{,}144{,}000$); #22 sums $\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}\left(\tfrac{3+4i}7\right)^n$ ($\tfrac7{16}$); #24 rotates $60^\circ$ about $B$ ($\angle APB=150^\circ$, $s=\sqrt7$); #25 finds the solutions $\tfrac{30}{29}n$, $n\le28$ ($\tfrac{29}{900}$, $929$).
- **Step D: 2021 Spring AMC 12B finished (2026-10-23).** The $15$ unwritten problems written and checked by script, with figures for #11 and #14: #11 by Stewart's Theorem ($PB=8\sqrt2$) and two pairs of similar triangles ($DE=\tfrac32PB=12\sqrt2$); #13 shows each crossing is unique where $\lvert5\cos3\theta\rvert\le4$ ($6$); #14 by the 3D British Flag Theorem, $h^2=(a-6)(a+2)$ ($24\sqrt5$); #15 splits the pentagon at $A$ ($\sqrt{11}+\sqrt{12}$); #17 solves $r^2-4r+2=0$ ($2+\sqrt2$); #18 becomes $(r-6)^2+(s+2)^2=0$ ($-2$); #21 brackets the second root between $2$ and $4$; #23 sums two geometric series ($\tfrac6{49}$); #24 uses $PQ=AC\cos\theta$, $RS=BD\cos\theta$ ($d^2=32+8\sqrt{41}$, $81$).
- **Markdown review, part 2 (2026-10-23).** PLAN's road ahead marks B and C done, gives step D's real counts (567 AMC 12, 650 AMC 8, 350 AJHSME left; every AMC 10 and AIME problem written), and adds D2 (the error check) and D3 (LIVE-guided labelling) as the owner asked; its housekeeping names the new helpers and the cloud index rebuild. CONTRIBUTING's write-up section describes the per-kind headings instead of the retired five-heading template, Key forms as a formula section, today's em-dash figures, and where candidate cards go now. MIGRATION-NOTES marks LIVE and the index rebuild as working from the cloud. CLAUDE.md records three standing decisions from today.
- **Eleven approved cards (P16 to P26), and a markdown review begun (2026-10-23).** Written as approved: Composing Transformations as Matrix Products (with a figure), Taking a Matrix Power by Diagonalizing, Dimension Arguments (Oddtown), Summing a Trigonometric Series with $e^{i\theta}$ (example $\sum\binom6k\cos\tfrac{k\pi}3=-27$), Independent Events (with a paragraph showing the product rule is the multiplication principle divided by the total, as the owner asked), Estimating to Choose an Answer, Palindromes, Counting the Shapes in a Figure, Fencepost Counting, Reading Graphs and Tables, and Using a Derivative to Find an Extremum. The method group Determinants is renamed Matrices & Determinants. Fourteen problems cite the new cards, their AMC 10/12 twins included (`tools/add-tag.js`). The search index was rebuilt again: 738 cards, 161/187 top-1. The owner asked for a full read-through of the markdown files: the README's intro counts, search note, Problems and Settings descriptions and file map, and CONVENTIONS' stale counts, card-order rule, stored keys (with the progress export) and Connected formulas section are brought up to date.
- **Ramsey Number $R(3,3)=6$ removed (owner, 2026-10-23).** The card, its write-up, example, Friends and Strangers widget, list entries and register rows are gone; its old address opens the Pigeonhole Principle (`MERGED_IDS`). 2021 Fall AMC 10B #23, its one problem, now cites the Pigeonhole Principle: four edges at a vertex in two colors, and three of one color would close a one-color triangle. Schur's Theorem mentions Ramsey's theorem without a link.
- **Step D: 2021 Spring AMC 12A finished (2026-10-23).** The $12$ unwritten problems written and checked by script, with a figure for #24: #8 has period $7$ mod $2$ ($(E,O,E)$); #13 compares $32\cos5\theta$ ($-\sqrt3+i$); #15 turns the condition into $t+(8-b)\equiv0\pmod4$ and sums $\binom{14}m$ ($4095$, remainder $95$); #19 makes both angles lie where sine is one-to-one ($2$ solutions); #20 solves $3p^2-40p+41=0$ ($\tfrac{40}3$); #21 fits the ellipse through the five roots ($e^2=\tfrac16$); #24 uses the radical axis of the two circles, and only $t=4$ keeps $P$ on the diameter ($\tfrac{99\sqrt3}{20}$, $122$).
- **Search index rebuilt; 2022 AMC 12B finished (2026-10-23).** The semantic index is rebuilt in the cloud for the first time (model2vec 0.9.0 distilling `BAAI/bge-base-en-v1.5`, 256 dimensions, 17,785 words, all 728 cards), so the newest cards are found by meaning as well as by words; the search eval gives 161/187 top-1, top-3 97.3%, MRR 0.917, cold and warm alike. The $12$ unwritten problems of 2022 AMC 12B written and checked by script, with figures for #10 and #19: #9 forces $a_7=32$ ($a_2=12$); #12 subtracts the two failure cases ($\tfrac{61}{81}$); #17 forces the array once the sums are placed ($24\cdot24=576$); #19 by complex coordinates ($\cos C=\tfrac{5\sqrt{13}}{26}$, $44$); #24 sums $\lvert1-\omega^k\rvert^4$ to $42$ ($147$); #25 cuts four kites $9-5\sqrt3$ from a square of side $2\sqrt3-1$ ($16\sqrt3-23$, $-4$).
- **Step D: 2022 AMC 12A finished (2026-10-23).** The $14$ unwritten problems written and checked by script, with figures for 2025 AMC 12B #10 and #25 and 2022 AMC 12A #12: #3 pairs the outer lengths to the side $8$, leaving $B$ ($4\times2$) in the middle; #10 assigns partners from $7$ down ($1\cdot2\cdot3\cdot4\cdot3\cdot2\cdot1=144$); #16 by the Pell equation $x^2-2y^2=1$ ($t_{288}=204^2$, $18$); #17 reduces to $a(1+2c)=4c^2-1$ ($-4$); #21 sends $x^{1011}$ to $x^3$ on the primitive ninth roots; #22 finds an isosceles trapezoid of area $\tfrac{99}{20}\sin2\theta$ ($c=\sqrt{20}$); #23 checks which primes divide the numerator ($8$); #25 counts $(a-2r)(b-2r)=2r^2$ and $(2r-a)(2r-b)=2r^2$ ($r=6$, ratio $17$). New tools: `tools/near-twins.js` (the near-twin scan) and `tools/show.js` (print statements). A figure file that failed to load (an import missing from `problem-diagrams.js`) was caught by `check-diagrams.py` before commit.
- **A pattern card, two settings moved, and proposals (2026-10-23).** New pattern A Quadratic That Must Be a Perfect Square (Patterns > Number Theory > Perfect-Square Conditions, a new family): $x^2+bx+c=y^2 \iff (2x+b)^2-(2y)^2=b^2-4c$, then factor pairs of matching parity; 1999 AIME #3, 2017 AIME II #6 and 2024 AMC 10A #11 cite it. Formula lines and Keyword tags moved into Reading the math in Settings. `QUESTIONS.md` has eleven proposals waiting for approval: four linear-algebra and complex-number methods (P16 to P19) and seven concepts from LIVE's tags that no card holds (P20 to P26), found by matching all 258 LIVE concepts against the cards. LIVE is reachable from the cloud now, and every paper's concept tags are cached in `tools/live-cache/`. The search index could not be rebuilt: the model weights come from `us.aws.cdn.hf.co` and `cas-server.xethub.hf.co`, which this environment's network policy refuses.
- **Completeness batch 2 (2026-10-23).** Six new cards, each with a figure (geometry), a write-up, and an example checked by script: Angle Between a Line and Its Rotated Image (a rotation by $\theta$ turns every line by $\theta$, proved with the feet of the perpendiculars from the center; AIME Early, low), Spheres Tangent to Two Planes (centers on one bisecting plane, $r=d\tan\tfrac\varphi2$; AIME Late, lower), Lester's Theorem, the Droz-Farny Line Theorem, the Anti-Steiner Point (with its directed-angle proof), and the Kruskal–Katona Theorem (seven triples cover at least $9$ pairs, checked by brute force). 2026 AIME I #10 now cites the rotated-line card (the rotation angle is the angle between $AC$ and $A'C'$) and 2026 AIME I #12 the two-planes card. 2023 AMC 10B #7 keeps its isosceles-triangle route: the rotated line $EF$ does cross $AB$ at $20^\circ$, but that does not reach $\angle EAB=35^\circ$ without the same isosceles triangle, so the card is not tagged there. Every figure was rendered and checked: one layout fix each for the spheres (radii now in ratio above $\tfrac{1+\sin20^\circ}{1-\sin20^\circ}$ so they sit apart), the anti-Steiner point, and the Lester circle.
- **Completeness batch 1, and three card fixes (2026-10-23).** Five new olympiad cards, each with a write-up, a full proof, and a computed example: Popoviciu's Inequality (with Hlawka's inequality as the case $f(t)=\lvert t\rvert$, proved by majorization and Karamata), the Vornicu–Schur Inequality, Cohn's Irreducibility Criterion (the root bound $\tfrac{1+\sqrt{37}}2$; example $1307 \to x^3+3x^2+7$), the Gauss–Lucas Theorem (example: the critical points of $z(z-6)(z-6i)$ are $(2\pm\sqrt2)+(2\mp\sqrt2)i$), and the Mason–Stothers Theorem (with Fermat for polynomials). Newton–Maclaurin already had a card (Newton's & Maclaurin's Inequalities), so none was added. Ptolemy on an Equilateral Triangle now states its converse (from Ptolemy's Inequality: $PA=PB+PC$ puts $P$ on the arc) and is the "Alternatively" route of 2025 AMC 12B #25, where $3=1+2$ puts the center on the circumcircle and $\angle AOB=120^\circ$ gives $s^2=7$. Bicentric Polygons & Poncelet Closure explains Poncelet's porism (any two nested conics, all or nothing, and why). First-Step Analysis (States), the Markov chain card, gains a paragraph on transition matrices: $v_n=v_0M^n$, eigenvalues, and $(I-Q)E=\mathbf 1$, with the cube walk's closed form $\tfrac14(1-3^{1-n})$ for odd $n$. New helpers: `tools/add-cards.py` (inserts a batch of cards, write-ups, examples, and registrations), `tools/add-to-list.py`, and `tools/check-pages.js` (the browser check).
- **Settings, Practice and links (2026-10-23).** Card order is now Curated or Most used (By contest tier is gone; a saved "tier" reads back as Curated). Keyword tags moved to Appearance. A new Practice panel holds Strategy: Shown, or Click to show, which keeps a problem's strategy and its cards behind a button. A new Your progress panel exports everything the app keeps in this browser (lists, stars, settings, theme, recently opened pages, flashcard and quiz progress) as one versioned JSON file (`competition-math-reference/progress`, version 1), and imports it back: lists are merged, the rest replaced. The settings preview uses cards with several formula rows, a sigma, and figures, so Formula lines, Notation and Figures on cards visibly change it; it shows the page-tools column when Page tools is on, and a fourth screen, a problem page, for the Strategy setting. Used by methods, Suggested by patterns and the Related formulas rows are now blue links with the hover preview, not boxes. Practice lists contests as AMC 8, AMC 10, AMC 12, AIME (so do a card's practice problems). 2025 AMC 10A #9 no longer cites Cardano's Cubic Formula: the line $y=25$ meets the cubic three times because its local extremes are $\pm\tfrac{200\sqrt3}9\approx\pm38.5$ (Counting Solutions by Graphing). Hook Length Formula, Pisano Periods and Ramsey $R(3,3)$ move from Lowest to Lower, since each has a tagged problem (CONVENTIONS, Tiers). New helpers: `tools/gates.sh` (every gate in order) and `tools/bump-versions.py --changed`.

A scan of tiers against levels and of importance against tagged problems raised three calls, C1 to C3, now in `QUESTIONS.md` (Pitot's tier, six AMC-tier cards studied on the AIME route, eight high-importance cards with no tagged problem yet).

Twelve that symbols would shorten are proposed in `QUESTIONS.md` (B1 to B12) and wait for approval.

Directed Angles gains the concyclicity test the box shows.

Named now: $c$ in Cauchy's templates, the roots and leading coefficient in the Fundamental Theorem of Algebra, $a_0$ and $a_n$ in the Rational Root Theorem, the degrees, leading coefficients and roots behind the resultant, $r$ and $\theta$ in polar form, $P$, $n$, $p$, $q$ in compound growth, the digit sum $S(n)$ and $s(n)$, $\mathbf 1$ and $\mathrm{id}$ in Dirichlet convolution, $\operatorname{ord}_n(a)$, the Frobenius number $g(a,b)$, the Fermat numbers $F_n$, $S(n,k)$ and $p_k(n)$ in the Twelvefold Way, $[n]$, Burnside's $G$ and $\mathrm{Fix}(g)$, Pólya's $m$ and $c(g)$, $\zeta(2)$, Hall's $N(S)$ and the largest degree $\Delta$.
- **Markdown files reorganized (2026-10-18).** `PLAN.md` now reads top to bottom: Current state, The road ahead (in order: 2.7c, 2.4e-ii, the card-file re-check, write-ups for every problem without one by AMC 10, AMC 12, AMC 8, then Stage R), how the practice work is done, blockers, where things are, the philosophy and standing rules, settled decisions, housekeeping, and an appendix of finished work. The per-chat list of finished cards is gone from the plan (this changelog has it), as are the overnight-run note and the twin-card item (settled 2026-10-12: the twins stay). `QUESTIONS.md` opens with what is open now (nothing) before the answered record. This changelog is regrouped by date, newest first. Cyclic Quadrilateral with Perpendicular Diagonals gains the Key form that the arcs cut off by opposite sides sum to $180^\circ$, and its converse.
- **Practice pass: Basic Probability finished (2026-10-18).** The last eleven strategies state their results. 2023 AIME I #1 used the factors $\tfrac9{13},\tfrac7{11},\ldots$, whose product is $\tfrac3{143}$, not the answer; the right chain is $\tfrac9{13}\cdot\tfrac8{11}\cdot\tfrac79\cdot\tfrac67=\tfrac{48}{143}$ ($191$). 2025 AIME I #7 is now written out by $G$'s partner ($\tfrac{128}{693}$).
- **Practice pass: conditional, binomial and hypergeometric probability (2026-10-18).** Eleven strategies written in full and brute-forced: 2017 AIME II #9 by the two shapes of a covering set ($\tfrac49$); 2010 AIME I #4 by the generating function $\tfrac{3+10x+11x^2+4x^3}{28}$ ($\tfrac{123}{392}$); 2014 AIME I #11 by the coordinates $x\pm y$ ($\tfrac{135}{256}$).
- **Practice pass: counting finished (2026-10-18).** The last eleven strategies written out and brute-forced, so every problem citing a formula card in any subject is now checked; only the methods and patterns in `patterns.js` remain. 2016 AIME I #13 solves the height recursion as a quadratic ($273$); 1986 AIME #12 bounds the set at five elements by pigeonhole ($56$ subsets, sums up to $54$) and builds $\{15,14,13,11,8\}$; 2022 AMC 10B #14 pairs against the largest element ($13$).
- **Practice pass: methods and patterns begun (2026-10-18).** The first 32 cards of `patterns.js` (Joining the Centers through Tangent Lines from a Point) are checked: 21 strategies written out or completed and brute-forced. 2023 AIME II #14 finds the edge from $\sin^2+\cos^2=1$ ($s=6$) and slices perpendicular to $AB$ (dry fraction $\tfrac{13}{96}$, $\tfrac{747}4$); 2022 AIME II #11 shows $\angle B=\angle C$ by the two reflections, then three similar triangles and Heron ($6\sqrt5$); 2024 AIME II #12 is the double root of the family equation ($C=(\tfrac18,\tfrac{3\sqrt3}8)$); 2003 AIME II #14 pins the heights by their sum ($f+g=8$) and rules out the degenerate case; 1999 AIME #2 goes through the parallelogram's center $(19,99)$. New app figures for 2020 AIME I #1, 2022 AIME II #11 and 2025 AMC 10A #6.
- **Practice pass: the algebra methods in `patterns.js` (2026-10-18).** 25 strategies written out and brute-forced, from `symmetric-polynomial-strategies` to `functional-substitution`. 2015 AIME I #10 now proves which three points share a sign (equal triple sums, $\{1,5,6\}$ and $\{2,3,7\}$); 2017 AMC 12A #25 splits size from direction ($a=8$, $b=4$, then the filter gives $\tfrac14$); 2016 AIME I #12 rules out $2,3,5,7$ by residues and $14641$ by completing the square ($m=132$); 2022 AIME II #6 had the wrong indices ($x_{10}-x_1$; it is $x_{76}-x_{16}$, $\tfrac{41}{800}$); 2008 AIME I #13 reduces to the family $a_7q_1+a_9q_2$ and $(19x-5)(x+1)=0$.
- **Practice pass: the number-theory methods in `patterns.js` (2026-10-18).** 20 strategies written out and brute-forced (`cycle-hunting` through `bounding-diophantine`). 2020 AIME II #6 said the period was nine; it is five ($t_6=20$, $t_7=21$), so $t_{2020}=t_5=\tfrac{101}{525}$. 2000 AIME I #4 reads the nine squares off the figure as $a$, $b$ combinations and closes with $5a=2b$ ($69\times61$); 2001 AIME I #11 solves $c_2\equiv7\pmod{31}$ by a modular inverse ($N=149$); 2019 AIME II #4 counts by $N_k=5^{k-1}+N_{k-1}$ ($200$ of $1296$); 2010 AIME II #10 is $\tfrac{324-2}2+2=163$.
- **Practice pass: counting methods, first part (2026-10-18).** 41 strategies written out and brute-forced (`coloring-arguments` through `symmetry-probability`). 2015 AIME I #9 claimed $|a_2-a_1|\le1$ or $|a_3-a_2|\le1$ was the whole condition; that gives $480$, and the missing $14$ triples have $a_3=1$, $|a_2-a_1|=2$ ($494$). 2020 AIME I #5 now removes its overcount ($36-5-5=26$, doubled). 2023 AMC 10B #10 / 12B #5 use the checkerboard (every domino covers one edge-center) and four disjoint dominoes for the lower bound. 2026 AIME I #11 shows the checkerboard total $5588-1692=3896$; 2017 AIME II #11 builds $S_5=544$ from $S_3=2$, $S_4=24$; 2006 AIME II #7 is now direct casework ($576+81+72+9$).
- **Practice pass: casework (2026-10-18).** All 49 open Casework problems written out and brute-forced, plus double counting and the gap method. Errors fixed: 2019 AIME II #10 used $\theta=\tfrac{360}7$ (it is $\tfrac{540}7$, period $3$ from $8\theta\equiv\theta$); 2023 AIME II #11 claimed every family shares an element (there are $5+76=81$, via upward closure and intersecting edge sets of $K_5$); 2019 AIME II #5 gave each block an orientation (the ambassador's even chair forces it, $105\cdot24$); 2007 AIME I #2 called Cy the fastest (Bob is, at $10$ ft/s). New full routes: 2026 AIME I #15 by the corner loop, $g(5,5)=27+2\cdot28=83$; 2024 AMC 10A #25 / 12A #22 by the two crossings, $2^6+2\cdot2^5+2^4+2=146$; 2025 AIME II #3 by the interior cross ($82$).
- **Practice pass 2.7a finished (2026-10-18).** The rest of `patterns.js` written out and brute-forced: constructive counting through the restricted-sequence cards (2004 AIME I #15 as compositions of $19$, $m=2^{18}-1280-256=2^9\cdot509$; 2004 AIME II #15 tracked fold by fold to $593$; 2023 AMC 12B #23 by $\sum\tfrac{(m+1)(3m+2)}2=936$; 2022 AIME I #7 rules out every larger denominator). Fourteen geometry strategies that ended in a bare answer now state their last step. Every card file reports 0 unchecked citations.
- **2.7c begun: every symbol defined on its own card (2026-10-18).** Geometry cards 1 to 75 read for symbols used but not defined in the thumbnail or description. The Exradii card used $A$ for both the area and an angle; the area is now $K$ on the card, its Key forms and its example. Points and lengths now named on Pythagoras ($\angle C$), Median to the Hypotenuse ($m_{\text{hyp}}$, $R$), Similar Figure Ratios ($\ell$, $A$, $V$), Triangle Similarity, Midsegment and Intercept ($D$, $E$, $M$, $N$), Viviani ($P$, $d_i$, $h$), the angle-bisector, incircle/excircle and trapezoid cards; the descriptions that repeated a thumbnail formula while defining its symbols now just define them.
- **2.7c: geometry finished (2026-10-18).** All 249 cards of `geometry.js` read. Undefined symbols now named on the card itself: the circumradius $R$, inradius $r$ and semiperimeter $s$ on the triangle-center, derived-triangle and identity cards (Euler line, nine-point circle, Feuerbach, Carnot, orthic, medial, contact, excentral, anticomplementary, Morley, Fact 5); the points and lines behind power of a point, chords, tangents, the radical axis, Monge, Sawayama–Thébault and the corner chain; the foci $F_1$, $F_2$ and focal distance $c$ on the conic cards; and $V$, $E$, $F$, $R$, $r$ on the solids. A new `tools/card-text.js` prints a file's cards 25 at a time for this pass.

**2026-10-17**

- **Four new cards from the practice pass (2026-10-17):** Angle Between the Diagonals from the Sides ($2d_1d_2\cos\theta=|a^2+c^2-b^2-d^2|$, so $\tan\theta=\tfrac{4K}{|a^2+c^2-b^2-d^2|}$), Flank Triangles of the Squares on a Triangle (each gap has the triangle's area and a third side of $2m_a$), Equilateral Hexagon with Opposite Sides Parallel ($\tfrac1s=\tfrac1a+\tfrac1b+\tfrac1c$), and Product of Two Uniform Variables ($P(XY\le c)=c-c\ln c$). Each has a write-up, an example, and a figure, and joins its route; the four problems that needed them now cite them. A fifth candidate, conjugating both sides of a complex equation, is proposed in QUESTIONS.md.
- **New method card, Conjugating Both Sides (2026-10-17):** take the conjugate of an equation to get a second one, with $\bar z=\tfrac1z$ on the unit circle and $w=\bar w$ for a real $w$. Three problems now cite it. 2018 AIME I #6 factors as $(z^{840}+1)(z^{600}-1)=0$, giving $1440$. 2001 AIME II #14 reduces to $z^{12}=-1$ and $z^4=\operatorname{cis}(\pm60^\circ)$, giving $840$. 2020 AMC 12B #23 is tagged for the first time.
- **New method card, Closing a Polygon with Side Vectors (2026-10-17):** the side vectors of a closed polygon add to zero. For an equiangular polygon this is $\sum a_k\omega^k=0$; its components give the linear relations, and dotting the sum with itself gives the side products. 2026 AIME I #14 cites it. QUESTIONS.md now lists the newest section first, with the newest rows at the top of each table.
- **Angle markers that run past their arms, root cause and gate (2026-10-17):** `angleArc` used a fixed radius without looking at the arms. `check-diagrams.py` now fails an angle arc that does not end on a drawn line. It found two: 2023 AIME I #12, now drawn with a ray past the short arm $FP$, and `roots-of-unity`, now with the radius to $\omega$ drawn. The rule is in CONVENTIONS §4.
- **Practice pass: Law of Cosines at 60° and 120° finished (2026-10-17).**
  - 2017 AMC 10B #19 and its twin 2017 AMC 12B #15 (untagged until now) share one entry: $\angle B'BC'=120^\circ$ gives $B'C'^2=37$. The alternative cuts off three corner triangles of $12$ times the area each (Shared-Angle Area Ratio).
  - 1987 AIME #9 drops Fermat Point: the route uses only the three $120^\circ$ angles, never the minimizing property.
  - 1995 AIME #14 adds Special Right Triangles and Area of a Triangle Using Sine, and states each step ($FP=9$, pieces $48$ and $30$, third side $42$).
  - 2022 AIME I #14 is written in full: each splitting line is parallel to an angle bisector (Angle Bisector Theorem and the Intercept Theorem), $\angle BIC=90^\circ+\tfrac A2$ forces $A=120^\circ$, and a mod 9 argument reduces $b^2+bc+c^2=219^2$ to $x^2+xy+y^2=73^2$, so the perimeter is $459$. The alternative solves it with Eisenstein integers. Casework is dropped.
  - Figures drawn for 2017 AMC 10B #19 (shared with 12B #15), 1995 AIME #14 and 2022 AIME I #14.
- **Practice pass: Special Triangles from the Law of Cosines finished (2026-10-17).** 2013 AIME II #5 cites the $60^\circ$ form instead of the general Law of Cosines, and Area Ratios from a Cevian instead of the same-base card (the three triangles share the apex $A$ and have equal bases); the strategy names the $2$-$3$-$\sqrt7$ triangle.

  - 1983 AIME #15 gets a full route: the midpoints of chords from $A$ lie on the circle with diameter $AO$, which must touch $BC$; with $BC$ on $y=-4$ this gives $A=(-4,-3)$ and $\sin=\tfrac7{25}$. Circle basics and power of a point are dropped.

- **Practice pass: Circular Segment & Lens Areas finished (2026-10-17).** 2015 AIME I #15 stopped before its answer. It now finds the projection, the disc minus two $120^\circ$ segments ($12\pi+18\sqrt3$), and divides by $\cos\theta=\tfrac35$, giving $20\pi+30\sqrt3$ and $53$. Affine Transformations is dropped; Projected Area is the step. A cylinder figure shows the cut. The other six problems were re-checked and needed no change.
- **Practice pass: Power of a Point finished (2026-10-17):** five vague strategies written in full.
  - 2001 AIME II #6: the chord through $F$ perpendicular to $CD$ gives $s(1+s)=\tfrac{(1-s)(1+s)}4$, so $s=\tfrac15$. The quadratic formula tag is dropped.
  - 2016 AIME I #15: tangent-chord angles and the circle $ABCD$ make $ADY$, $YAB$, $YCB$ similar, so $YC\cdot YD=AB^2$; $XY$ bisects $\angle CXD$, and the bisector length gives $AB^2=67\cdot37-47^2=270$ (found and checked on a numerical model).
  - 2022 AIME I #11: tangent lengths $6$ and $20$, and the diameter between the parallel sides gives $\cos A=\tfrac{6-x}{6+x}$; with $AC=28$, $x=\tfrac92$ and the area is $147\sqrt3$.
  - 2023 AIME II #9: $A$ and $B$ sit over the midpoints of $XP$ and $PY$, so $AB=12$; $MP(MP+5)=36$ gives $h=\sqrt{15}$ and area $18\sqrt{15}$.
  - 2024 AIME I #5: the power of $E$ gives $e(e+184)=17\cdot33$, so $CE=104$. Ptolemy is dropped.
  - Figures drawn for 2001 AIME II #6, 2016 AIME I #15 and 2023 AIME II #9.
- **Practice pass: Length of a Chord finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2023 AIME I #5 is now Thales and areas: $P$ is $\tfrac{56}d$ and $\tfrac{90}d$ from the diagonals, so $d^4=44944$ and the area is $106$. Ptolemy and the chord card are dropped.
  - 1985 AIME #9 states $k^2=\tfrac{256}{15}$ and the two cosines $\tfrac{11}{16}$, $\tfrac{14}{16}$.
  - 2023 AMC 12B #20 explains the answer correctly: the start lies on the second circle, and the good points form an arc of $2\theta$, $\theta=2\arcsin\tfrac14$.
  - Figures drawn for 1985 AIME #9 and 2023 AIME I #5; the second figure's self-check caught a misplaced $P$ before commit.
- **Practice pass: Equal Chords, Arcs & Distances and the Inscribed Angle Theorem finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2011 AIME I #10 counts obtuse triangles from their first vertex: $\tfrac{3(k-1)}{2(2k-1)}$ for $n=2k+1$ and $\tfrac{3(k-2)}{2(2k-1)}$ for $n=2k$, giving $127+376=503$.
  - 2015 AIME I #6: $\tfrac32(m-a)=12^\circ$ and $5m=180^\circ-2a$ give $a=20^\circ$, $m=28^\circ$, so $\angle BAG=10^\circ+48^\circ=58^\circ$.
  - 2017 AIME I #6: $2u^2+(1-2u)^2=\tfrac{11}{25}$ gives $x=84^\circ$ or $36^\circ$.
  - 2021 AIME II #14: $AXOG$ and $OGMY$ are cyclic, so $\angle XOY=\angle XAM+\angle AMY=180^\circ-(B-C)=A+2C$ (checked on three triangles), giving $A=\tfrac{585}7$. The cyclic-quadrilateral tag is replaced by the tangent-chord angle.
  - Figures drawn for 1991 AIME #14 and 2021 AIME II #14.
- **Practice pass: Thales' Theorem, Angles from Chords and Tangent Line Facts finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2017 AIME I #8: $QR=200\sin|a-b|$, so $QR\le100$ means $|a-b|\le30$ and the probability is $\tfrac{16}{25}$ (checked by simulation).
  - 2000 AIME II #10: $\tan(\alpha+\beta)=-\tan(\gamma+\delta)$ gives $105r^2=67935$, $r^2=647$.
  - 2002 AIME II #15: the radii are the roots of $k^2r^2-(18k+12)r+117=0$, so $\tfrac{117}{k^2}=68$ and $m=\tfrac{12\sqrt{221}}{49}$ (Vieta and the double angle formula).
  - 2007 AIME I #9: tangent lengths $\tfrac r4$ and $\tfrac{3r}5$, so $\tfrac{57r}{20}=34$.
  - 2007 AIME II #9: $EP=378-364=14$ and $EQ=378-105=273$, so $PQ=259$; it cites Incircle Tangent Lengths.
  - 2023 AIME I #8: $r=\tfrac{25}2$, the projections $\tfrac72$ and $\tfrac{15}2$ give $\cos A=\tfrac35$, and the perimeter is $125$.
  - 2025 AMC 10A #20 cites the distance method for tangent lines.
  - Figures drawn for 2017 AIME I #8, 2025 AMC 10A #20, 2000 AIME II #10, 2007 AIME I #9, 2007 AIME II #9 and 2023 AIME I #8.
- **Practice pass: the tangent-chord angle, radical axis, Apollonius circle, Descartes, tangent circles and common tangents cards finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2019 AIME I #15: the homotheties at $A$ and $B$ make $OO_1PO_2$ a parallelogram, so $OQ\perp PQ$, $Q$ is the midpoint of $XY$, and $PQ^2=\tfrac{121-60}4=\tfrac{61}4$.
  - 2021 AIME I #13: linearity of power gives $672r=2d\cdot\tfrac r2$, so $d=672$.
  - 2015 AIME II #15: in coordinates $\ell$ has slope $\tfrac32$, $AD=\tfrac4{\sqrt{13}}$, and the area is $\tfrac{64}{65}$; the common-tangent card is dropped (not used).
  - 2005 AIME II #15: the centers lie on $\tfrac{x^2}{100}+\tfrac{(y-12)^2}{75}=1$, and tangency gives $a^2=\tfrac{69}{100}$.
  - 2023 AMC 12B #10 states the slopes ($-\tfrac52$, so $\tfrac25$).
  - Figures drawn for 2019 AIME I #15 and 2021 AIME I #13.
- **Practice pass: cyclic-quadrilateral cards, Ptolemy, Pitot and Distance & Midpoint finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2001 AIME I #13: Ptolemy on the trapezoid gives $(c+20)^2=484+22c$, so $c=-9+\sqrt{165}$.
  - 2020 AIME II #4: a $90^\circ$ clockwise turn about $(21,-3)$, so the answer is $108$.
  - 2021 AIME I #6: $BP^2+CP^2+DP^2-GP^2=2AP^2$, so $AP=192$ (the old text had a garbled scaling step).
  - 2022 AIME I #1: $P+Q$ is the line through $(16,108)$ and $(20,106)$, so it cites Forms of a Line, not distance and midpoint.
  - Figure drawn for 2001 AIME I #13.
- **Practice pass: Forms of a Line, Angle Between Lines, Shoelace and Pick's Theorem finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2005 AIME I #10: $|980-56p|=140$ gives $p=15$ and $p+q=47$.
  - 2009 AIME I #11: the determinant is $2009(x_1-x_2)$, so the count is $2\binom{25}2=600$.
  - 2017 AIME II #10: $O=(12,24)$, $[BCON]=2184$, $P=(32,29)$, $[CDP]=546$.
  - 2019 AIME I #3 is now the sine area formula on the three corners ($150-10-7.5-12.5=120$), and 2025 AMC 12A #17 is base times height; neither cites the shoelace formula.
  - The AMC 10A/12A/12B line and angle problems state their numbers.
  - Figures drawn for 2017 AIME II #10 and 2019 AIME I #3.
- **Practice pass: point-line distance through rotations finished (2026-10-17):** ten vague strategies written in full and checked by computation.
  - 2024 AIME I #9: $\tfrac1{OA^2}+\tfrac1{OB^2}=\tfrac1{120}$, so $BD^2\gt480$.
  - 2020 AIME II #7: $r=\tfrac{15}{\sqrt{73}}$.
  - 2025 AIME I #9: the image is the reflection in $y=-\sqrt3x$ (two reflections make the rotation), so the point is on that line; it cites Composing Two Reflections.
  - 2021 AIME I #15: the combination $2(\text{first})+(\text{second})$ is a circle of radius$^2$ $\tfrac{325}{16}+\tfrac{3k}2$, so $k\le280$, with four intersections from $k=5$.
  - 2011 AIME I #3: the signed distances give $\alpha+\beta=\tfrac{403}{13}=31$.
  - 2000 AIME I #2: the area is $u(u+3v)=11\cdot41$, so $u+v=21$ (the old text had a placeholder).
  - 2005 AIME I #14: equal perpendicular segments in a square give $E=(2,-2)$ and $K=\tfrac{1936}{10}$.
  - 2010 AIME I #11: two cones, volume $\tfrac{343\pi}{12\sqrt{10}}$.
  - 2017 AIME II #12: $B=\tfrac{1-r}{1-ri}$, $|B|=\tfrac{49}{61}$ (checked by simulation).
  - 2017 AIME I #15: the third vertex on the hypotenuse is $\tfrac7{20}p+\tfrac{11\sqrt3}{60}q=1$, so the side squared is $\tfrac{300}{67}$.
- **Practice pass: solid-geometry cards through the insphere finished (2026-10-17):**
  - 2023 AMC 12B #21: the shade unrolls to a half-annulus of radii $6$ and $12$; the shortest path is a tangent ($6\sqrt3$) plus an arc ($\pi$).
  - 1993 AIME #10: casework on the vertex degree leaves only $d=4$, $V=30$, $T=P=2$.
  - 1999 AIME #15 now uses the isosceles tetrahedron ($pqr=1224$, $V=408$).
  - 2001 AIME I #12 ($r=\tfrac23$), 2026 AIME II #12 ($RS=\tfrac{175}{48}$), 2003 AIME I #5 ($\tfrac{462+40\pi}3$), 2012 AIME I #8 ($\tfrac16-\tfrac1{48}$, so $\tfrac{41}{48}$) and 2023 AIME I #13 (Gram determinants $\tfrac{3969}2$ and $1922$, ratio $\tfrac{63}{62}$) are written out with their numbers.
  - Figures drawn for 2023 AMC 12B #21 and 2026 AIME II #12.
  - Four candidate cards, P8 to P11, added to QUESTIONS.md.
- **Practice pass: geometry finished (2026-10-17).** The last cards (3D distance through cross-ratio) had ten vague strategies, now written in full: 2023 AMC 10B #17 and 12B #13 share one entry ($\tfrac94$); 2024 AIME I #15 finds the extreme box $2\times2\times\tfrac{23}4$ ($r^2=\tfrac{657}{64}$); 2011 AIME I #13 solves $3k^2-66k+265=0$; 1998 AIME #11 and 2007 AIME I #13 get their cross-sections from the shadow area over $\cos\theta$ ($525$ and $\sqrt{80}$); 2006 AIME I #14 gives $h=\tfrac{144}{\sqrt{1585}}$; 2017 AIME I #10 finds the circle's center at real part $56$; 2026 AIME I #12 puts the centers on the bisecting plane ($r=\tfrac{122}{39}$); 1991 AIME #11 has $r=\tan15^\circ$.
- **Five new geometry cards from the practice pass (approved P7 to P11, 2026-10-17):** Cevian Ratio Sum ($\tfrac{PD}{AD}+\tfrac{PE}{BE}+\tfrac{PF}{CF}=1$), Perpendicular Segments Across a Square, Splitting Lines (Cleavers), Perpendicular Radii of a Central Conic, and Volume from Dot Products (Gram Determinant). Each has a full write-up with proof, a verified example, a figure with a self-check, a place in the AIME geometry route (now 89 cards) and a new-cards batch. 1988 AIME #12, 2005 AIME I #14, 2022 AIME I #14, 2024 AIME I #9 and 2023 AIME I #13 now cite them.
- **Owner's fixes (2026-10-17):** the home page has a plain gear (no box) at its top right that opens Settings. Under Your library, "Make a study list" and "Open a random card" became "Make a list" (opens Practice with the cursor in the new-list box) and "Open a list" (opens Practice at the lists), since a random problem is already linked under Practice at a glance. Circumradius of a Cyclic Quadrilateral gains a third key form, $R=\tfrac14\sqrt{\tfrac{(ab+cd)(ac+bd)(ad+bc)}{(s-a)(s-b)(s-c)(s-d)}}$ (checked on the $7$-$24$-$20$-$15$ quadrilateral, $R=12.5$). The Arbelos moves to lowest importance and now says that $a$ and $b$ are the radii of the small semicircles and where $D$ is. PLAN.md gains step 2.7c: after the problem pass, check every card for variables that are not defined. On a phone (640 px or narrower) the home page drops the line under the title.
- **Practice pass: algebra begun, Quadratic Formula card finished (2026-10-17).** All 32 problems re-checked; thirteen strategies written out with their numbers (among them 2023 AMC 12A #16 by $w=z+\tfrac12$ and a sign argument on a quadratic in $a^2$, and 2021 AIME II #7 rejecting the branch with no real roots), and the quadratic-formula tag dropped where no quadratic is solved (1998 #9, 2006 II #5, 2007 I #8). 2019 HMMT February Geometry #9 gets its first strategy.
- **Practice pass: Vieta's Formulas (Quadratic) finished, (General Degree) begun (2026-10-17).** All 18 quadratic-Vieta problems checked; nine vague strategies written in full with their numbers (2018 AIME I #1, $S=2600$; 2020 AIME I #11, $441+69$; 2020 AIME I #14, $49+36$; 2020 AIME II #11, $\alpha=\tfrac32$, $R(0)=\tfrac{52}{19}$; 2025 AIME I #11, $\tfrac{1+5\sqrt{185}}{68}$). 2019 AIME II #6 never forms a quadratic and drops the Vieta tag; 2023 AMC 12A #19 now says the product of the solutions comes from the *sum* of the roots in $\log_{2023}x$, which is $0$ since $7\cdot289=2023$. The seven AMC problems of the general-degree card are done (2021 Spring AMC 12A #22 gains product-to-sum and the telescoping cosine product; 2024 AMC 12A #23 uses the quartic $x^4-28x^3+70x^2-28x+1$ and $70-2=68$).
- **Practice pass: Vieta's Formulas (General Degree) finished (2026-10-17).** All 32 problems checked; the 14 vague AIME strategies written in full. 2025 AIME II #15 now has a complete route: $N(x)-nx=(x-\alpha)^2(x-\beta)^2$, and comparing coefficients with $k=2v^2$ gives $(v+16)(v-2)(v-4)(v-10)=0$, so $k=8,32,200$; it cites Writing the Tangency Equation instead of Turning Known Values into Known Roots. 2003 AIME II #9 shows the remainder $x^2-x+1$; 2010 AIME II #7 uses $P(1)=-135$; 2013 AIME I #10 lists its 16 pairs; 2014 AIME II #5 solves $r^2+4r-5=0$; 2019 AIME I #10 reaches $-\tfrac{343}9$; 2001 AIME I #3 shows its two coefficients. Newton's Sums is finished as well: 2019 AIME I #8 runs $P_k=P_{k-1}-pP_{k-2}$ to $p=\tfrac16$ and $P_6=\tfrac{13}{54}$, and drops the binomial theorem.
- **Practice pass: polynomial cards through Cardano finished (2026-10-17).** Fundamental Theorem of Algebra, Factor and Remainder, Rational Root, Coefficient Extraction, Conjugate Roots, Vertex Form and Cardano. 2007 AIME II #14 was wrong ($n=3$, $26^3$) and now proves every root is $\pm i$, so $f=(x^2+1)^2$ and $f(5)=676$. 2005 AIME II #13 uses $(x-17)(x-24)R(x)$, not a constant multiple, and drops Bounding; 2020 AIME II #10 rejects $n=327$ (remainder $100$); 2023 AIME I #9 counts $8+10$ pairs; 2002 AIME II #11 explains the ratio $1-r$; 2018 AIME II #6 factors out $x=1$ and $x=-2$; 2016 AIME II #6 evaluates $Q(-1)=\left(\tfrac32\right)^5$.
- **Owner's card changes (2026-10-17):** Splitting Lines (Cleavers) has a shorter formula box, $MX \parallel CD,\ AX = \tfrac{a+b}2\ (a \le b)$, and its description now names $X$, $a$, $b$ and the bisector $CD$. Perpendicular Segments Across a Square is now a pattern (Patterns > Geometry > Polygon Configurations), keeping its id, figure and problem. Its first method to try is Extending Two Sides to Meet: extended to the lines of the sides, a slanted segment makes right triangles that are all similar. Coordinates come next, and Rotating a Point, Triangle Similarity, the distance between parallel lines and Pythagoras are the formulas to try.
- **Practice pass: Difference of Squares & Cubes finished (2026-10-17).** 16 vague strategies written in full. 2008 AIME II #15 had the wrong square assigned: $2n+1=a^2$ fails mod $3$, so $2n-1=a^2$, and $a^2+80=k^2$ gives $a=19$, $n=181$ (Pell's equation is no longer cited; Quadratic Residues and factor pairs are). 2003 AIME II #10 now rejects $b=196$ because $c=900$ is a square; 2005 AIME I #12 counts $a=990$; 2025 AIME II #4 telescopes to $3\cdot\tfrac{31}{13}$; 2023 AMC 10A #15 compares $n=62$ and $64$.
- **Practice pass: Sophie Germain through the Binomial Theorem (2026-10-17).** 2010 AIME I #9 never uses $a^3+b^3+c^3-3abc$: naming $p=xyz$ gives $7p^2+43p+60=0$ and $\tfrac{151}7$, so it cites Substituting for a Repeated Block, and 2024 AIME II #11 is now $100q-abc=2\cdot10^6-xyz$ (Rewriting in Symmetric Sums), so the $a^3+b^3+c^3-3abc$ card cites no problem. 2026 AIME I #13 is corrected: $(1+x)^{10000}\equiv(1+x)^{462}$ modulo $503$ and $x^{502}-1$, so the $39$ multiples are $S_{463}$ to $S_{501}$, not vanishing coefficients (it no longer cites the roots-of-unity filter). 2006 AIME I #12 cites the sum of cubes instead of $(a+b+c)^2$; 1987 AIME #14 shows the telescoping to $\tfrac{3730}{10}$; 1995 AIME #13 adds the last block of $210$ terms.
- **Practice pass: Arithmetic Series finished (2026-10-17).** All 54 problems checked; 27 vague strategies written in full and checked by script. Among them: 2024 AMC 10A #21 / 12A #14 writes every cell as $p+qi+rj+sij$ ($29$); 2016 AIME I #10 shows the odd terms' square roots are arithmetic, so $a_{13}=14\cdot12^2$ and $a_1=504$; 2020 AIME II #8 sums the zeros as $n\left(\tfrac{n(n-1)}2+1\right)$; 2006 AIME II #13 lists its $5+10$ values of $N$; 2022 AIME I #6 removes $48$ pairs; 2013 AIME I #15 sums $32+30+\cdots+2$; 1998 AIME #5 makes each block of four $-2$.
- **Practice pass: Geometric Series finished (2026-10-17).** 20 strategies written in full with their numbers (2013 AIME I #14 reaches $\tfrac PQ=\tfrac{\cos\theta}{2+\sin\theta}$ and $57s^2+32s-17=0$; 2008 AIME II #9 gives $z_{150}=-5\sqrt2+(5+5\sqrt2)i$; 1993 AIME #11 runs $p_{n+1}=\tfrac23-\tfrac13p_n$; 2004 AIME I #12 multiplies $\tfrac23\cdot\tfrac56$). 2023 AIME I #12 now names Carnot's Perpendicularity Theorem and Viviani's Theorem at the steps that use them (both checked numerically: $BX+CY+AZ=82.5$, $\tan^2\theta=75$). Four strategies showed a stray backslash before an apostrophe ("Carnot\\'s"); fixed, and `tools/practice-set.js` now refuses one.
